\displaystyle R({\bm{w}}_{t-1})+\frac{1}{2\eta}\left[\|\bar{{\bm{w}}}-{\bm{w}}_{t-1}\|^{2}-2\langle\eta{\bm{g}}_{t-1},{\bm{w}}_{t-1}-\bar{{\bm{w}}}\rangle\right]-\frac{1}{2\eta}\|{\bm{w}}_{t}-\bar{{\bm{w}}}\|^{2}+\frac{\sqrt{\beta}}{2\eta}\|{\bm{v}}_{t}-\bar{{\bm{w}}}\|^{2}

\displaystyle+\left[\frac{L_{s}-\frac{1}{\eta}}{2}\right]\|{\bm{w}}_{t}-{\bm{w}}_{t-1}\|^{2}+\langle[\nabla R({\bm{w}}_{t-1})-{\bm{g}}_{t-1}]_{F},{\bm{w}}_{t}-{\bm{w}}_{t-1}\rangle

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{=}

\displaystyle R({\bm{w}}_{t-1})+\frac{1}{2\eta}\|\bar{{\bm{w}}}-{\bm{w}}_{t-1}\|^{2}-\langle{\bm{g}}_{t-1},{\bm{w}}_{t-1}-\bar{{\bm{w}}}\rangle-\frac{1}{2\eta}\|{\bm{w}}_{t}-\bar{{\bm{w}}}\|^{2}+\frac{\sqrt{\beta}}{2\eta}\|{\bm{v}}_{t}-\bar{{\bm{w}}}\|^{2}

\displaystyle+\left[\frac{L_{s}-\frac{1}{\eta}+C}{2}\right]\|{\bm{w}}_{t}-{\bm{w}}_{t-1}\|^{2}+\frac{1}{2C}\|[\nabla R({\bm{w}}_{t-1})-{\bm{g}}_{t-1}]_{F}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle R({\bm{w}}_{t-1})+\frac{1}{2\eta}\|\bar{{\bm{w}}}-{\bm{w}}_{t-1}\|^{2}-\langle\nabla R({\bm{w}}_{t-1}),{\bm{w}}_{t-1}-\bar{{\bm{w}}}\rangle+\langle\nabla R({\bm{w}}_{t-1})-{\bm{g}}_{t-1},{\bm{w}}_{t-1}-\bar{{\bm{w}}}\rangle

\displaystyle--\frac{1}{2\eta}\|{\bm{w}}_{t}-\bar{{\bm{w}}}\|^{2}+\frac{\sqrt{\beta}}{2\eta}\|{\bm{v}}_{t}-\bar{{\bm{w}}}\|^{2}+\left[\frac{L_{s^{\prime}}-\frac{1}{\eta}+C}{2}\right]\|{\bm{w}}_{t}-{\bm{w}}_{t-1}\|^{2}+\frac{1}{2C}\|[\nabla R({\bm{w}}_{t-1})-{\bm{g}}_{t-1}]_{F}\|^{2},

where (a) follows from Lemma
3.2
and (b) follows from the inequality
\langle a,b\rangle\leq\frac{C}{2}a^{2}+\frac{1}{2C}b^{2}
, for any
(a,b)\in(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{2}
with
C>0
an arbitrary strictly positive constant.

Let us now assume that
\eta:=\frac{1}{L_{s^{\prime}}+C}
: therefore the term
\left[\frac{L_{s^{\prime}}-\frac{1}{\eta}+C}{2}\right]\|{\bm{w}}_{t}-{\bm{w}}_{t-1}\|^{2}
above is
0
. We now take the conditional expectation (conditioned on
{\textnormal{w}}_{t-1}
, which is the random variable which realizations are
{\bm{w}}_{t-1}
), on both sides, and from Lemma
F.3

we obtain the inequality below (we slightly abuse notations and denote
\mathbb{E}[\cdot|{\textnormal{w}}_{t-1}={\bm{w}}_{t-1}]
by
\mathbb{E}[\cdot|{\bm{w}}_{t-1}]
):

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[R({\bm{w}}_{t})|{\bm{w}}_{t-1}]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle R({\bm{w}}_{t-1})+\frac{1}{2\eta}\|\bar{{\bm{w}}}-{\bm{w}}_{t-1}\|^{2}-\langle\nabla R({\bm{w}}_{t-1}),{\bm{w}}_{t-1}-\bar{{\bm{w}}}\rangle

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2\eta}\mathbb{E}\left[\|{\bm{w}}_{t}-\bar{{\bm{w}}}\|^{2}|{\bm{w}}_{t-1}\right]+\frac{\sqrt{\beta}}{2\eta}\mathbb{E}\left[\|{\bm{v}}_{t}-\bar{{\bm{w}}}\|^{2}|{\bm{w}}_{t-1}\right]+\langle[\nabla R({\bm{w}}_{t-1})-\mathbb{E}\left[{\bm{g}}_{t-1}|{\bm{w}}_{t-1}]\right]_{F},{\bm{w}}_{t-1}-\bar{{\bm{w}}}\rangle

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{2C}\|[\nabla R({\bm{w}}_{t-1})-{\bm{g}}_{t-1}]_{F}\|^{2}|{\bm{w}}_{t-1}\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\leq}

\displaystyle R({\bm{w}}_{t-1})+\frac{1}{2\eta}\|\bar{{\bm{w}}}-{\bm{w}}_{t-1}\|^{2}-\langle\nabla R({\bm{w}}_{t-1}),{\bm{w}}_{t-1}-\bar{{\bm{w}}}\rangle

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2\eta}\mathbb{E}\left[\|{\bm{w}}_{t}-\bar{{\bm{w}}}\|^{2}|{\bm{w}}_{t-1}\right]+\frac{\sqrt{\beta}}{2\eta}\mathbb{E}\left[\|{\bm{v}}_{t}-\bar{{\bm{w}}}\|^{2}|{\bm{w}}_{t-1}\right]+

\displaystyle\frac{G}{2}\|[\nabla R({\bm{w}}_{t-1})-\mathbb{E}[{\bm{g}}_{t-1}|{\bm{w}}_{t-1}]]_{F}\|^{2}+\frac{1}{2G}\|{\bm{w}}_{t-1}-\bar{{\bm{w}}}\|^{2}+\frac{1}{2C}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\nabla R({\bm{w}}_{t-1})-{\bm{g}}_{t-1}\|^{2}|{\bm{w}}_{t-1}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle R({\bm{w}}_{t-1})+\left[\frac{1}{2\eta}+\frac{1}{2G}\right]\|\bar{{\bm{w}}}-{\bm{w}}_{t-1}\|^{2}-\langle\nabla R({\bm{w}}_{t-1}),{\bm{w}}_{t-1}-\bar{{\bm{w}}}\rangle

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2\eta}\mathbb{E}\left[\|{\bm{w}}_{t}-\bar{{\bm{w}}}\|^{2}|{\bm{w}}_{t-1}\right]+\frac{\sqrt{\beta}}{2\eta}\mathbb{E}\left[\|{\bm{v}}_{t}-\bar{{\bm{w}}}\|^{2}|{\bm{w}}_{t-1}\right]+\frac{G}{2}\|[\nabla R({\bm{w}}_{t-1})-\mathbb{E}[{\bm{g}}_{t-1}|{\bm{w}}_{t-1}]]_{F}\|^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2C}\mathbb{E}\left[\|[\nabla R({\bm{w}}_{t-1})-{\bm{g}}_{t-1}]_{F}\|^{2}|{\bm{w}}_{t-1}\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{\leq}

\displaystyle R({\bm{w}}_{t-1})+\left[\frac{1}{2\eta}+\frac{1}{2G}\right]\|\bar{{\bm{w}}}-{\bm{w}}_{t-1}\|^{2}-\langle\nabla R({\bm{w}}_{t-1}),{\bm{w}}_{t-1}-\bar{{\bm{w}}}\rangle

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2\eta}\mathbb{E}\left[\|{\bm{w}}_{t}-\bar{{\bm{w}}}\|^{2}|{\bm{w}}_{t-1}\right]+\frac{\sqrt{\beta}}{2\eta}\mathbb{E}\left[\|{\bm{v}}_{t}-\bar{{\bm{w}}}\|^{2}|{\bm{w}}_{t-1}\right]+\frac{G}{2}\|[\nabla R({\bm{w}}_{t-1})-\mathbb{E}[{\bm{g}}_{t-1}|{\bm{w}}_{t-1}]]_{F}\|^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2C}\left(2\|[\nabla R({\bm{w}}_{t-1})-\mathbb{E}[{\bm{g}}_{t-1}|{\bm{w}}_{t-1}]]_{F}\|^{2}+2\|[{\bm{g}}_{t-1}-\mathbb{E}[{\bm{g}}_{t-1}|{\bm{w}}_{t-1}]]_{F}\|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(\ref{eq:bias})+(\ref{eq:variance})}{\leq}

\displaystyle R({\bm{w}}_{t-1})+\left[\frac{1}{2\eta}+\frac{1}{2G}\right]\|\bar{{\bm{w}}}-{\bm{w}}_{t-1}\|^{2}-\langle\nabla R({\bm{w}}_{t-1}),{\bm{w}}_{t-1}-\bar{{\bm{w}}}\rangle

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2\eta}\mathbb{E}\left[\|{\bm{w}}_{t}-\bar{{\bm{w}}}\|^{2}|{\bm{w}}_{t-1}\right]+\frac{\sqrt{\beta}}{2\eta}\mathbb{E}\left[\|{\bm{v}}_{t}-\bar{{\bm{w}}}\|^{2}|{\bm{w}}_{t-1}\right]+\frac{G}{2}C_{3}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2C}\left(2C_{3}+2C_{1}\|\nabla R({\bm{w}}_{t-1})\|^{2}+2C_{2}\mu^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(c)}{\leq}

\displaystyle R({\bm{w}}_{t-1})+\left[\frac{1}{2\eta}+\frac{1}{2G}\right]\|\bar{{\bm{w}}}-{\bm{w}}_{t-1}\|^{2}-\langle\nabla R({\bm{w}}_{t-1}),{\bm{w}}_{t-1}-\bar{{\bm{w}}}\rangle

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2\eta}\mathbb{E}\left[\|{\bm{w}}_{t}-\bar{{\bm{w}}}\|^{2}|{\bm{w}}_{t-1}\right]+\frac{\sqrt{\beta}}{2\eta}\mathbb{E}\left[\|{\bm{v}}_{t}-\bar{{\bm{w}}}\|^{2}|{\bm{w}}_{t-1}\right]+\frac{G}{2}C_{3}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2C}\left(2C_{1}\left(2\|\nabla R({\bm{w}}_{t-1})-\nabla R(\bar{{\bm{w}}})\|^{2}+2\|\nabla R(\bar{{\bm{w}}})\|^{2}\right)+2C_{2}\mu^{2}+2C_{3}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(d)}{\leq}

\displaystyle R({\bm{w}}_{t-1})+\left[\frac{1}{2\eta}+\frac{1}{2G}\right]\|\bar{{\bm{w}}}-{\bm{w}}_{t-1}\|^{2}-\langle\nabla R({\bm{w}}_{t-1}),{\bm{w}}_{t-1}-\bar{{\bm{w}}}\rangle

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2\eta}\mathbb{E}\left[\|{\bm{w}}_{t}-\bar{{\bm{w}}}\|^{2}|{\bm{w}}_{t-1}\right]+\frac{\sqrt{\beta}}{2\eta}\mathbb{E}\left[\|{\bm{v}}_{t}-\bar{{\bm{w}}}\|^{2}|{\bm{w}}_{t-1}\right]+\frac{G}{2}C_{3}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2C}\left(2C_{1}\left(2L_{s^{\prime}}^{2}\|{\bm{w}}_{t-1}-\bar{{\bm{w}}}\|^{2}+2\|\nabla R(\bar{{\bm{w}}})\|^{2}\right)+2C_{2}\mu^{2}+2C_{3}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle R({\bm{w}}_{t-1})+\left[\frac{1}{2\eta}+\frac{1}{2G}+\frac{2C_{1}L_{s^{\prime}}^{2}}{C}\right]\|\bar{{\bm{w}}}-{\bm{w}}_{t-1}\|^{2}-\langle\nabla R({\bm{w}}_{t-1}),{\bm{w}}_{t-1}-\bar{{\bm{w}}}\rangle

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2\eta}\mathbb{E}\left[\|{\bm{w}}_{t}-\bar{{\bm{w}}}\|^{2}|{\bm{w}}_{t-1}\right]+\frac{\sqrt{\beta}}{2\eta}\mathbb{E}\left[\|{\bm{v}}_{t}-\bar{{\bm{w}}}\|^{2}|{\bm{w}}_{t-1}\right]+\frac{G}{2}C_{3}+\frac{1}{C}\left(2C_{1}\|\nabla R(\bar{{\bm{w}}})\|^{2}+C_{2}\mu^{2}+C_{3}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(e)}{\leq}

\displaystyle R({\bm{w}}_{t-1})+\left[\frac{1}{2\eta}+\frac{1}{2G}+\frac{2C_{1}L_{s^{\prime}}^{2}}{C}\right]\|\bar{{\bm{w}}}-{\bm{w}}_{t-1}\|^{2}+\left[R(\bar{{\bm{w}}})-R({\bm{w}}_{t-1})-\frac{\nu_{s}}{2}\|{\bm{w}}_{t-1}-\bar{{\bm{w}}}\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2\eta}\mathbb{E}\left[\|{\bm{w}}_{t}-\bar{{\bm{w}}}\|^{2}|{\bm{w}}_{t-1}\right]+\frac{\sqrt{\beta}}{2\eta}\mathbb{E}\left[\|{\bm{v}}_{t}-\bar{{\bm{w}}}\|^{2}|{\bm{w}}_{t-1}\right]+\frac{G}{2}C_{3}+\frac{1}{C}\left(2C_{1}\|\nabla R(\bar{{\bm{w}}})\|^{2}+C_{2}\mu^{2}+C_{3}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle R(\bar{{\bm{w}}})+\left[\frac{\frac{1}{\eta}-\nu_{s}}{2}+\frac{1}{2G}+\frac{2C_{1}L_{s^{\prime}}^{2}}{C}\right]\|\bar{{\bm{w}}}-{\bm{w}}_{t-1}\|^{2}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2\eta}\mathbb{E}\left[\|{\bm{w}}_{t}-\bar{{\bm{w}}}\|^{2}|{\bm{w}}_{t-1}\right]+\frac{\sqrt{\beta}}{2\eta}\mathbb{E}\left[\|{\bm{v}}_{t}-\bar{{\bm{w}}}\|^{2}|{\bm{w}}_{t-1}\right]+\frac{G}{2}C_{3}+\frac{1}{C}\left(2C_{1}\|\nabla R(\bar{{\bm{w}}})\|^{2}+C_{2}\mu^{2}+C_{3}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(f)}{\leq}

\displaystyle R(\bar{{\bm{w}}})+\left[\frac{\frac{1}{\eta}-\nu_{s}}{2}+\frac{1}{2G}+\frac{2\varepsilon_{F}L_{s^{\prime}}^{2}}{\tau C}\right]\|\bar{{\bm{w}}}-{\bm{w}}_{t-1}\|^{2}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2\eta}\mathbb{E}\left[\|{\bm{w}}_{t}-\bar{{\bm{w}}}\|^{2}|{\bm{w}}_{t-1}\right]+\frac{\sqrt{\beta}}{2\eta}\mathbb{E}\left[\|{\bm{v}}_{t}-\bar{{\bm{w}}}\|^{2}|{\bm{w}}_{t-1}\right]+\frac{G}{2}C_{3}+\frac{1}{C}\left(2C_{1}\|\nabla R(\bar{{\bm{w}}})\|^{2}+C_{2}\mu^{2}+C_{3}\right)

(48)

Where (a) follows from the inequality
\langle a,b\rangle\leq\frac{G}{2}a^{2}+\frac{1}{2G}b^{2}
, for any
(a,b)\in(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{2}
with
G>0
an arbitrary strictly positive constant,
(b) and (c) follow from the inequality
\|a+b\|^{2}\leq 2\|a\|^{2}+2\|b\|^{2}
for any
(a,b)\in(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{2}
, (d) follows from the fact that
R
is
(L_{s^{\prime}},s^{\prime})
-RSS’ (Assumption
4.5
with sparsity level
s^{\prime}
), therefore it is also (
L_{s^{\prime}}
,
s
)-RSS’, (e) follows from the RSC condition, and for (f), we recall that
C_{1}=\frac{\varepsilon_{F}}{q_{t}}
, and we define
q_{t}=\left\lceil\frac{\tau}{\omega^{t}}\right\rceil
, for some
\omega>1
and
\tau>0
that will be chosen later in the proof.

Recall that we have chosen
\eta:=\frac{1}{L_{s^{\prime}}+C}
.
Let us define
\alpha:=\frac{C}{L_{s^{\prime}}}+1
. Then
C=(\alpha-1)L_{s^{\prime}}
, and
\eta=\frac{1}{\alpha L_{s^{\prime}}}
. Also recall that
\kappa_{s}=\frac{L_{s^{\prime}}}{\nu_{s}}
.

We will now choose the constant
G
and
C
, in order to simplify the inequality above, such that it matches as much as possible the structure of the previous proofs:

We will seek to rewrite:

\left[\frac{\frac{1}{\eta}-\nu_{s}}{2}+\frac{1}{2G}+\frac{2\frac{\varepsilon_{F}}{\tau}L_{s^{\prime}}^{2}}{C}\right]\left(=\frac{1}{2\eta}\left[1+\frac{1}{G\alpha L_{s^{\prime}}}+\frac{4L_{s^{\prime}}^{2}\frac{\varepsilon_{F}}{\tau}}{(\alpha-1)\alpha L_{s^{\prime}}^{2}}-\frac{1}{\alpha\kappa_{s}}\right]\right)
, into :

\frac{1}{2\eta}\left[1-\frac{1}{\alpha^{\prime}\kappa_{s}}\right]
for some
\alpha^{\prime}>0
(we will seek
\alpha^{\prime}\propto\alpha
, with a dimensionless proportionality constant for simplicity).

Therefore, let us choose
G:=\frac{4}{\nu_{s}}
, which implies:

\frac{1}{G\alpha L_{s^{\prime}}}=\frac{1}{4\alpha\kappa_{s}}.

(49)

And let us choose
\tau:=\frac{16\kappa_{s}\varepsilon_{F}}{(\alpha-1)}
, which implies:

\frac{4L_{s^{\prime}}^{2}\frac{\varepsilon_{F}}{\tau}}{(\alpha-1)\alpha L_{s^{\prime}}^{2}}=\frac{1}{4\alpha\kappa_{s}}.

(50)

Therefore, using equations
49
and
50
, we obtain:

\displaystyle\left[\frac{\frac{1}{\eta}-\nu_{s}}{2}+\frac{1}{2G}+\frac{2\frac{\varepsilon_{F}}{\tau}L_{s^{\prime}}^{2}}{C}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\eta}\left[1+\frac{1}{G\alpha L_{s^{\prime}}}+\frac{4L_{s^{\prime}}^{2}\frac{\varepsilon_{F}}{\tau}}{(\alpha-1)\alpha L_{s^{\prime}}^{2}}-\frac{1}{\alpha\kappa_{s}}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\eta}\left[1+\frac{1}{4\alpha\kappa_{s}}+\frac{1}{4\alpha\kappa_{s}}-\frac{1}{\alpha\kappa_{s}}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\eta}\left[1-\frac{1}{2\alpha\kappa_{s}}\right]=\frac{1}{2\eta}\left[1-\frac{1}{\alpha^{\prime}\kappa_{s}}\right],

where for simplicity we denote
\alpha^{\prime}=2\alpha
.

We can therefore simplify (
48
) into:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[R({\bm{w}}_{t})|{\bm{w}}_{t-1}]-R(\bar{{\bm{w}}})\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\eta}\left[\left(1-\frac{1}{\alpha^{\prime}\kappa_{s}}\right)\|\bar{{\bm{w}}}-{\bm{w}}_{t-1}\|^{2}-\frac{1}{2\eta}\mathbb{E}\left[\|{\bm{w}}_{t}-\bar{{\bm{w}}}\|^{2}|{\bm{w}}_{t-1}\right]\right.

\displaystyle+\frac{\sqrt{\beta}}{2\eta}\mathbb{E}\left[\|{\bm{v}}_{t}-\bar{{\bm{w}}}\|^{2}|{\bm{w}}_{t-1}\right]

\displaystyle\left.+2\eta\left(\frac{G}{2}C_{3}+\frac{1}{C}\left(2C_{1}\|\nabla R(\bar{{\bm{w}}})\|^{2}+C_{2}\mu^{2}+C_{3}\right)\right)\right].

We now take the expectation over
{\textnormal{w}}_{t-1}
of the above inequality (i.e. we take
\mathbb{E}_{{\textnormal{w}}_{t-1}}[\cdot]
): using the law of total expectation (
\mathbb{E}[\cdot]=\mathbb{E}_{{\textnormal{w}}_{t-1}}[\mathbb{E}[\cdot|{\bm{w}}_{t-1}]]
) we obtain:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}R({\bm{w}}_{t})-R(\bar{{\bm{w}}})\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\eta}\left[\left(1-\frac{1}{\alpha^{\prime}\kappa_{s}}\right)\mathbb{E}\|\bar{{\bm{w}}}-{\bm{w}}_{t-1}\|^{2}-\frac{1}{2\eta}\mathbb{E}\left[\|{\bm{w}}_{t}-\bar{{\bm{w}}}\|^{2}\right]\right.

(51)

\displaystyle+\frac{\sqrt{\beta}}{2\eta}\mathbb{E}\left[\|{\bm{v}}_{t}-\bar{{\bm{w}}}\|^{2}\right]

(52)

\displaystyle\left.+2\eta\left(\frac{G}{2}C_{3}+\frac{1}{C}\left(2C_{1}\|\nabla R(\bar{{\bm{w}}})\|^{2}+C_{2}\mu^{2}+C_{3}\right)\right)\right].

(53)

Let us call
A:=2\eta\left(\frac{G}{2}C_{3}+\frac{1}{C}\left(2C_{1}\|\nabla R(\bar{{\bm{w}}})\|^{2}+C_{2}\mu^{2}+C_{3}\right)\right)
for simplicity.

This gives:

\mathbb{E}R({\bm{w}}_{t})-R(\bar{{\bm{w}}})\leq\frac{1}{2\eta}\left[\left(1-\frac{1}{\alpha^{\prime}\kappa_{s}}\right)\mathbb{E}\|\bar{{\bm{w}}}-{\bm{w}}_{t-1}\|^{2}-\frac{1}{2\eta}\mathbb{E}\|{\bm{w}}_{t}-\bar{{\bm{w}}}\|^{2}+\frac{\sqrt{\beta}}{2\eta}\mathbb{E}\|{\bm{v}}_{t}-\bar{{\bm{w}}}\|^{2}+A\right].

(54)

Additionally, in view of equation
40
applied at
{\bm{v}}_{t}
instead of
{\bm{w}}_{t}
, (since
{\bm{v}}_{t}
here corresponds to the
{\bm{w}}_{t}
from Section
F.2.2
, i.e.
{\bm{v}}_{t}
is the hard-thresholding of an iterate after a gradient step), we know that:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}R({\bm{v}}_{t})-R(\bar{{\bm{w}}})\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\eta}\left[\left(1-\frac{1}{\alpha^{\prime}\kappa_{s}}\right)\mathbb{E}\|\bar{{\bm{w}}}-{\bm{w}}_{t-1}\|^{2}-(1-\sqrt{\beta})\mathbb{E}\|{\bm{w}}_{t}-\bar{{\bm{w}}}\|^{2}+A\right].

We now take a convex combination similarly as in the case without additional constraint (section
E.2
), for some
\rho\in(0,1)
.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(1-\rho)R({\bm{w}}_{t})+\rho R({\bm{v}}_{t})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle R(\bar{{\bm{w}}})+\frac{1}{2\eta}\left[\left(1-\frac{1}{\alpha^{\prime}\kappa_{s}}\right)\mathbb{E}\|\bar{{\bm{w}}}-{\bm{w}}_{t-1}\|^{2}-(1-\rho)\mathbb{E}\|{\bm{w}}_{t}-\bar{{\bm{w}}}\|^{2}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\left((1-\rho)\sqrt{\beta}-(1-\sqrt{\beta})\rho\right)\mathbb{E}\|{\bm{v}}_{t}-\bar{\bm{w}}\|^{2}+A\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle R(\bar{{\bm{w}}})+\frac{1}{2\eta}\left[\left(1-\frac{1}{\alpha^{\prime}\kappa_{s}}\right)\mathbb{E}\|\bar{{\bm{w}}}-{\bm{w}}_{t-1}\|^{2}-(1-\rho)\mathbb{E}\|{\bm{w}}_{t}-\bar{{\bm{w}}}\|^{2}\right.

\displaystyle\left.-\left(\rho-\sqrt{\beta}\right)\mathbb{E}\|{\bm{v}}_{t}-\bar{\bm{w}}\|^{2}+A\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{\leq}

\displaystyle R(\bar{{\bm{w}}})+\frac{1}{2\eta}\left[\left(1-\frac{1}{\alpha^{\prime}\kappa_{s}}\right)\mathbb{E}\|\bar{{\bm{w}}}-{\bm{w}}_{t-1}\|^{2}-(1-\rho)\mathbb{E}\|{\bm{w}}_{t}-\bar{{\bm{w}}}\|^{2}\right.

\displaystyle\left.-\left(\rho-\sqrt{\beta}\right)\mathbb{E}\|{\bm{w}}_{t}-\bar{\bm{w}}\|^{2}+A\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle R(\bar{{\bm{w}}})+\frac{1}{2\eta}\left[\left(1-\frac{1}{\alpha^{\prime}\kappa_{s}}\right)\mathbb{E}\|\bar{{\bm{w}}}-{\bm{w}}_{t-1}\|^{2}-(1-\sqrt{\beta})\mathbb{E}\|{\bm{w}}_{t}-\bar{{\bm{w}}}\|^{2}+A\right].

where in (b), we have assumed that
\sqrt{\beta}\leq\rho
(later we will verify that our choice of
k
ensures such a condition), and have used the fact that projection onto a convex set is non-expansive (which implies that
\|{\bm{v}}_{t}-\bar{\bm{w}}\|^{2}\geq\|{\bm{w}}_{t}-\bar{\bm{w}}\|^{2}
).

Similarly as in
(
Liu & Foygel Barber 2020
)
, we now take a weighted sum over
t=1,...,T
, to obtain:

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{T}2\eta\left(\frac{1-\frac{1}{\alpha^{\prime}\kappa_{s}}}{1-\sqrt{\beta}}\right)^{T-t}\mathbb{E}[(1-\rho)R({\bm{w}}_{t})+\rho R({\bm{v}}_{t})-R(\bar{{\bm{w}}})]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\frac{1-\frac{1}{\alpha^{\prime}\kappa_{s}}}{1-\sqrt{\beta}}\right)^{T-t}\left[\left(1-\frac{1}{\alpha^{\prime}\kappa_{s}}\right)\mathbb{E}\|\bar{{\bm{w}}}-{\bm{w}}_{t-1}\|^{2}-(1-\sqrt{\beta})\mathbb{E}\|{\bm{w}}_{t}-\bar{{\bm{w}}}\|^{2}+A\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\frac{1-\frac{1}{\alpha^{\prime}\kappa_{s}}}{1-\sqrt{\beta}}\right)^{T-t}\left[\left(1-\frac{1}{\alpha^{\prime}\kappa_{s}}\right)\mathbb{E}\|\bar{{\bm{w}}}-{\bm{w}}_{t-1}\|^{2}-(1-\sqrt{\beta})\mathbb{E}\|{\bm{w}}_{t}-\bar{{\bm{w}}}\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle+\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\frac{1-\frac{1}{\alpha^{\prime}\kappa_{s}}}{1-\sqrt{\beta}}\right)^{T-t}A

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(1-\sqrt{\beta})\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left[\left(\frac{1-\frac{1}{\alpha^{\prime}\kappa_{s}}}{1-\sqrt{\beta}}\right)^{T-t+1}\mathbb{E}\|\bar{{\bm{w}}}-{\bm{w}}_{t-1}\|^{2}-\left(\frac{1-\frac{1}{\alpha^{\prime}\kappa_{s}}}{1-\sqrt{\beta}}\right)^{T-t}\mathbb{E}\|{\bm{w}}_{t}-\bar{{\bm{w}}}\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle+\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\frac{1-\frac{1}{\alpha^{\prime}\kappa_{s}}}{1-\sqrt{\beta}}\right)^{T-t}A

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{=}

\displaystyle(1-\sqrt{\beta})\left[\left(\frac{1-\frac{1}{\alpha^{\prime}\kappa_{s}}}{1-\sqrt{\beta}}\right)^{T}\|\bar{{\bm{w}}}-{\bm{w}}_{0}\|^{2}-\mathbb{E}\|{\bm{w}}_{T}-\bar{{\bm{w}}}\|^{2}\right]+\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\frac{1-\frac{1}{\alpha^{\prime}\kappa_{s}}}{1-\sqrt{\beta}}\right)^{T-t}A

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(1-\sqrt{\beta})\left(\frac{1-\frac{1}{\alpha^{\prime}\kappa_{s}}}{1-\sqrt{\beta}}\right)^{T}\|\bar{{\bm{w}}}-{\bm{w}}_{0}\|^{2}+\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\frac{1-\frac{1}{\alpha^{\prime}\kappa_{s}}}{1-\sqrt{\beta}}\right)^{T-t}A

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1-\frac{1}{\alpha^{\prime}\kappa_{s}}}{1-\sqrt{\beta}}\right)^{T}\|\bar{{\bm{w}}}-{\bm{w}}_{0}\|^{2}+\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\frac{1-\frac{1}{\alpha^{\prime}\kappa_{s}}}{1-\sqrt{\beta}}\right)^{T-t}A

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1-\frac{1}{\alpha^{\prime}\kappa_{s}}}{1-\sqrt{\beta}}\right)^{T}\|\bar{{\bm{w}}}-{\bm{w}}_{0}\|^{2}+\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\frac{1-\frac{1}{\alpha^{\prime}\kappa_{s}}}{1-\sqrt{\beta}}\right)^{T-t}2\eta\left(\frac{G}{2}C_{3}+\frac{1}{C}\left(2C_{1}\|\nabla R(\bar{{\bm{w}}})\|^{2}\right.\right.

\displaystyle~~~~\left.\left.+C_{2}\mu^{2}+C_{3}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1-\frac{1}{\alpha^{\prime}\kappa_{s}}}{1-\sqrt{\beta}}\right)^{T}\|\bar{{\bm{w}}}-{\bm{w}}_{0}\|^{2}+\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\frac{1-\frac{1}{\alpha^{\prime}\kappa_{s}}}{1-\sqrt{\beta}}\right)^{T-t}2\eta\left(\frac{G}{2}C_{3}+\frac{1}{C}\left(2\frac{\varepsilon_{F}}{q_{t}}\|\nabla R(\bar{{\bm{w}}})\|^{2}\right.\right.

\displaystyle~~~~\left.\left.+\frac{\varepsilon_{abs}\mu^{2}}{q_{t}}+C_{3}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1-\frac{1}{\alpha^{\prime}\kappa_{s}}}{1-\sqrt{\beta}}\right)^{T}\|\bar{{\bm{w}}}-{\bm{w}}_{0}\|^{2}+\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\frac{1-\frac{1}{\alpha^{\prime}\kappa_{s}}}{1-\sqrt{\beta}}\right)^{T-t}\frac{2\eta}{q_{t}}\left(\frac{2\varepsilon_{F}\|\nabla R(\bar{{\bm{w}}})\|^{2}+\varepsilon_{abs}\mu^{2}}{C}\right)

\displaystyle+\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\frac{1-\frac{1}{\alpha^{\prime}\kappa_{s}}}{1-\sqrt{\beta}}\right)^{T-t}2\eta C_{3}\left(\frac{G}{2}+\frac{1}{C}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1-\frac{1}{\alpha^{\prime}\kappa_{s}}}{1-\sqrt{\beta}}\right)^{T}\|\bar{{\bm{w}}}-{\bm{w}}_{0}\|^{2}+\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\frac{1-\frac{1}{\alpha^{\prime}\kappa_{s}}}{1-\sqrt{\beta}}\right)^{T-t}\frac{2\eta}{q_{t}}\left(\frac{2\varepsilon_{F}\|\nabla R(\bar{{\bm{w}}})\|^{2}}{C}\right)

\displaystyle+\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\frac{1-\frac{1}{\alpha^{\prime}\kappa_{s}}}{1-\sqrt{\beta}}\right)^{T-t}2\eta\mu^{2}\left(\varepsilon_{\mu}\left(\frac{G}{2}+\frac{1}{C}\right)+\frac{\varepsilon_{abs}}{Cq_{t}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1-\frac{1}{\alpha^{\prime}\kappa_{s}}}{1-\sqrt{\beta}}\right)^{T}\|\bar{{\bm{w}}}-{\bm{w}}_{0}\|^{2}+\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\frac{1-\frac{1}{\alpha^{\prime}\kappa_{s}}}{1-\sqrt{\beta}}\right)^{T-t}\frac{2\eta}{q_{t}}\left(\frac{2\varepsilon_{F}\|\nabla R(\bar{{\bm{w}}})\|^{2}}{C}\right)

\displaystyle+\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\frac{1-\frac{1}{\alpha^{\prime}\kappa_{s}}}{1-\sqrt{\beta}}\right)^{T-t}2\eta\mu^{2}\left(\varepsilon_{\mu}\left(\frac{G}{2}+\frac{1}{C}\right)+\frac{\varepsilon_{abs}}{C}\right),

(55)

where (a) follows from simplifying the telescopic sum.
Let us denote for simplicity
\zeta:=\frac{2\eta(2\varepsilon_{F}\|\nabla R(\bar{{\bm{w}}})\|^{2})}{C}=\frac{4\eta\varepsilon_{F}\|\nabla R(\bar{{\bm{w}}})\|^{2}}{C}
and
Z:=\varepsilon_{\mu}\left(\frac{G}{2}+\frac{1}{C}\right)+\frac{\varepsilon_{abs}}{C}
.

We now choose
k
and
s_{t}
as follows: we choose
k\geq 4\frac{\alpha^{\prime 2}}{\rho}\kappa_{s}^{2}\bar{k}
, which implies that:

\displaystyle\sqrt{\beta}\leq\frac{1}{2\frac{\alpha^{\prime}}{\rho}\kappa_{s}}

\displaystyle\implies\sqrt{\beta}\leq\frac{1}{2\frac{\alpha^{\prime}}{\rho}\kappa_{s}-1}

\displaystyle\implies 1-\sqrt{\beta}\geq 1-\frac{1}{2\frac{\alpha^{\prime}}{\rho}\kappa_{s}-1}=\frac{2\frac{\alpha^{\prime}}{\rho}\kappa_{s}-2}{2\frac{\alpha^{\prime}}{\rho}\kappa_{s}-1}=\frac{1-\frac{1}{\frac{\alpha^{\prime}}{\rho}\kappa_{s}}}{1-\frac{1}{2\frac{\alpha^{\prime}}{\rho}\kappa_{s}}}

\displaystyle\implies\left(\frac{1-\frac{1}{\frac{\alpha^{\prime}}{\rho}\kappa_{s}}}{1-\sqrt{\beta}}\right)\leq 1-\frac{1}{2\frac{\alpha^{\prime}}{\rho}\kappa_{s}}.

(56)

We recall that we previously defined
q_{t}=\left\lceil\frac{\tau}{\omega^{t}}\right\rceil
, with
\tau=16\kappa_{s}\frac{\varepsilon_{F}}{(\alpha-1)}
. We now set the value of
\omega
, to
\omega:=1-\frac{1}{\frac{\alpha^{\prime}}{\rho}\kappa_{s}}
.

Let us call
\nu:=1-\frac{1}{2\frac{\alpha^{\prime}}{\rho}\kappa_{s}}
. Note that we have:

\nu\leq\omega.

(57)

And that we have the inequality below:

\frac{\nu}{\omega}=\frac{1-\frac{1}{2\frac{\alpha^{\prime}}{\rho}\kappa_{s}}}{1-\frac{1}{4\frac{\alpha^{\prime}}{\rho}\kappa_{s}}}=\frac{4\frac{\alpha^{\prime}}{\rho}\kappa_{s}-2}{4\frac{\alpha^{\prime}}{\rho}\kappa_{s}-1}=1-\frac{1}{4\frac{\alpha^{\prime}}{\rho}\kappa_{s}-1}\leq 1-\frac{1}{4\frac{\alpha^{\prime}}{\rho}\kappa_{s}}=\omega.

(58)

This allows us to simplify equation
55
into:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\sum_{t=1}^{T}2\eta\left(\frac{1-\frac{1}{\alpha^{\prime}\kappa_{s}}}{1-\sqrt{\beta}}\right)^{T-t}[(1-\rho)R({\bm{w}}_{t})+\rho R({\bm{v}}_{t})-R(\bar{{\bm{w}}})]

\displaystyle\leq\nu^{T}\|\bar{{\bm{w}}}-{\bm{w}}_{0}\|^{2}+\sum_{t=1}^{T}\nu^{T-t}\omega^{t-1}+\frac{\zeta}{\tau}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\frac{1-\frac{1}{\alpha^{\prime}\kappa_{s}}}{1-\sqrt{\beta}}\right)^{T-t}2\eta Z\mu^{2}

\displaystyle=\nu^{T}\|\bar{{\bm{w}}}-{\bm{w}}_{0}\|^{2}+\frac{\omega^{T}}{\omega}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\frac{\nu}{\omega}\right)^{T-t}+\frac{\zeta}{\tau}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\frac{1-\frac{1}{\alpha^{\prime}\kappa_{s}}}{1-\sqrt{\beta}}\right)^{T-t}2\eta Z\mu^{2}

\displaystyle=\nu^{T}\|\bar{{\bm{w}}}-{\bm{w}}_{0}\|^{2}+\frac{\omega^{T}}{\omega}\frac{1-\left(\frac{\nu}{\omega}\right)^{T}}{1-\left(\frac{\nu}{\omega}\right)}+\frac{\zeta}{\tau}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\frac{1-\frac{1}{\alpha^{\prime}\kappa_{s}}}{1-\sqrt{\beta}}\right)^{T-t}2\eta Z\mu^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\nu^{T}\|\bar{{\bm{w}}}-{\bm{w}}_{0}\|^{2}+\frac{\omega^{T}}{\omega}\frac{1}{1-\left(\frac{\nu}{\omega}\right)}+\frac{\zeta}{\tau}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\frac{1-\frac{1}{\alpha^{\prime}\kappa_{s}}}{1-\sqrt{\beta}}\right)^{T-t}2\eta Z\mu^{2}

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\leq}\nu^{T}\|\bar{{\bm{w}}}-{\bm{w}}_{0}\|^{2}+\frac{\omega^{T}}{\omega}\frac{1}{1-\omega}+\frac{\zeta}{\tau}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\frac{1-\frac{1}{\alpha^{\prime}\kappa_{s}}}{1-\sqrt{\beta}}\right)^{T-t}2\eta Z\mu^{2}

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{\leq}\nu^{T}\|\bar{{\bm{w}}}-{\bm{w}}_{0}\|^{2}+\frac{4}{3}\omega^{T}\frac{1}{1-\omega}+\frac{\zeta}{\tau}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\frac{1-\frac{1}{\alpha^{\prime}\kappa_{s}}}{1-\sqrt{\beta}}\right)^{T-t}2\eta Z\mu^{2}

\displaystyle\overset{(c)}{\leq}\omega^{T}\|\bar{{\bm{w}}}-{\bm{w}}_{0}\|^{2}+\frac{4}{3}\omega^{T}\frac{1}{1-\omega}+\frac{\zeta}{\tau}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\frac{1-\frac{1}{\alpha^{\prime}\kappa_{s}}}{1-\sqrt{\beta}}\right)^{T-t}2\eta Z\mu^{2}

\displaystyle\overset{(d)}{\leq}\frac{\omega^{T}}{1-\omega}\|\bar{{\bm{w}}}-{\bm{w}}_{0}\|^{2}+\frac{4}{3}\omega^{T}\frac{1}{1-\omega}+\frac{\zeta}{\tau}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\frac{1-\frac{1}{\alpha^{\prime}\kappa_{s}}}{1-\sqrt{\beta}}\right)^{T-t}2\eta Z\mu^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\omega^{T}}{1-\omega}\left(\|\bar{{\bm{w}}}-{\bm{w}}_{0}\|^{2}+\frac{4}{3}\right)+\frac{\zeta}{\tau}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\frac{1-\frac{1}{\alpha^{\prime}\kappa_{s}}}{1-\sqrt{\beta}}\right)^{T-t}2\eta Z\mu^{2}

\displaystyle=4\frac{\alpha^{\prime}}{\rho}\kappa_{s}\omega^{T}\left(\|\bar{{\bm{w}}}-{\bm{w}}_{0}\|^{2}+\frac{4}{3}\right)+\frac{\zeta}{\tau}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\frac{1-\frac{1}{\alpha^{\prime}\kappa_{s}}}{1-\sqrt{\beta}}\right)^{T-t}2\eta Z\mu^{2},

where in the left hand side we have used the linearity of expectation, and where (a) uses equation
58
,
(b) uses the fact that
\frac{1}{\omega}=\frac{1}{1-\frac{1}{4\frac{\alpha^{\prime}}{\rho}\kappa_{s}}}\leq\frac{1}{1-\frac{1}{4}}=\frac{4}{3}
(since
\kappa_{s}\geq 1
and
\alpha^{\prime}\geq 1
(indeed, we have
\alpha^{\prime}=2\alpha=2(\frac{C}{L_{s^{\prime}}}+1)
with
C>0
), so consequently
\frac{\alpha^{\prime}}{\rho}\geq 1
), (c) uses equation
57
, and (d) uses the fact that
\omega<1
so
1<\frac{1}{1-\omega}
.

Let us now normalize the above inequality:

\mathbb{E}\frac{\sum_{t=1}^{T}2\eta\left(\frac{1-\frac{1}{\alpha^{\prime}\kappa_{s}}}{1-\sqrt{\beta}}\right)^{T-t}[(1-\rho)R({\bm{w}}_{t})+\rho R({\bm{v}}_{t})]}{\sum_{t=1}^{T}2\eta\left(\frac{1-\frac{1}{\alpha^{\prime}\kappa_{s}}}{1-\sqrt{\beta}}\right)^{T-t}}\leq R(\bar{{\bm{w}}})+\frac{4\frac{\alpha^{\prime}}{\rho}\kappa_{s}\omega^{T}\left(\|\bar{{\bm{w}}}-{\bm{w}}_{0}\|^{2}+\frac{4}{3}\frac{\zeta}{\tau}\right)}{\sum_{t=1}^{T}2\eta\left(\frac{1-\frac{1}{\alpha^{\prime}\kappa_{s}}}{1-\sqrt{\beta}}\right)^{T-t}}+Z\mu^{2}.

The left hand side above is a weighted sum, which is an upper bound on the smallest term of the sum.

Regarding the right hand side, we can simplify it using the fact that
0<\left(\frac{1-\frac{1}{\alpha^{\prime}\kappa_{s}}}{1-\sqrt{\beta}}\right)
, and therefore:

\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\frac{1-\frac{1}{\alpha^{\prime}\kappa_{s}}}{1-\sqrt{\beta}}\right)^{T-t}\geq 1.

Therefore, we obtain:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\min_{t\in\{1,..,T\}}[(1-\rho)R({\bm{w}}_{t})+\rho R({\bm{v}}_{t})-R(\bar{{\bm{w}}})]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4\frac{\alpha^{\prime}}{\rho}\kappa_{s}\omega^{T}\left(\|\bar{{\bm{w}}}-{\bm{w}}_{0}\|^{2}+\frac{4}{3}\frac{\zeta}{\tau}\right)}{2\eta}+Z\mu^{2}

\displaystyle=4\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\rho}L_{s^{\prime}}\kappa_{s}\omega^{T}\left(\|\bar{{\bm{w}}}-{\bm{w}}_{0}\|^{2}+\frac{4}{3}\frac{\zeta}{\tau}\right)+Z\mu^{2},

which can be simplified into the expression below, using the definition of
\hat{{\bm{w}}}_{T}
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\min_{t\in[T]}(1-\rho)R({\bm{w}}_{t})+\rho R({\bm{v}}_{t})-R(\bar{{\bm{w}}})]

\displaystyle\leq 4\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\rho}L_{s^{\prime}}\kappa_{s}\omega^{T}\left(\|\bar{{\bm{w}}}-{\bm{w}}_{0}\|^{2}+\frac{4}{3}\frac{\zeta}{\tau}\right)+Z\mu^{2}.

(59)

To simplify the above result, we recall the assumptions made earlier on:
we have chosen

\tau=\frac{16\kappa_{s}\varepsilon_{F}}{(\alpha-1)}
, and
G=\frac{4}{\nu_{s}}
.

Therefore, to sum up, we have:

Z=\varepsilon_{\mu}\left(\frac{G}{2}+\frac{1}{C}\right)+\frac{\varepsilon_{abs}}{C}=\varepsilon_{\mu}\left(\frac{2}{\nu_{s}}+\frac{1}{C}\right)+\frac{\varepsilon_{abs}}{C}

\omega=1-\frac{1}{4\frac{\alpha^{\prime}}{\rho}\kappa_{s}}=1-\frac{1}{8\frac{\alpha}{\rho}\kappa_{s}}

\zeta=\frac{4\eta\varepsilon_{F}\|\nabla R(\bar{{\bm{w}}})\|^{2}}{C}

The last inequality implies:
\frac{\zeta}{\tau}=\frac{\frac{4\eta\varepsilon_{F}\|\nabla R(\bar{{\bm{w}}})\|^{2}}{C}}{16\kappa_{s}L_{s^{\prime}}\frac{\varepsilon_{F}}{C}}=\frac{\eta\|\nabla R(\bar{{\bm{w}}})\|^{2}}{4\kappa_{s}L_{s^{\prime}}}
.

Let us denote by
\varepsilon_{T}
the right-hand side term from equation
59
:

\varepsilon_{T}=4\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\rho}L_{s^{\prime}}\kappa_{s}\omega^{T}\left(\|\bar{{\bm{w}}}-{\bm{w}}_{0}\|^{2}+\frac{4}{3}\frac{\eta\|\nabla R(\bar{{\bm{w}}})\|^{2}}{4\kappa_{s}L_{s^{\prime}}}\right)+Z\mu^{2}.

We now proceed similarly as in the proof of Theorem
4.3
above. Recall that we have assumed in the Assumptions of Theorem
4.8
, without loss of generality, that
R
is non-negative, which implies that
R\left({\bm{v}}_{t}\right)\geq 0
. Plugging this in equation
59
implies that:

\mathbb{E}\min_{t\in[T]}R\left({\bm{w}}_{t}\right)\leq\frac{1}{1-\rho}R(\bar{{\bm{w}}})+\frac{\varepsilon_{T}}{1-\rho}+\frac{Z}{(1-\rho)}\mu^{2}\leq(1+2\rho)R(\bar{{\bm{w}}})+\frac{\varepsilon_{T}}{1-\rho}+\frac{Z}{1-\rho}\mu^{2}.

(60)

Plugging the change of variable
\varepsilon^{\prime}_{T}=\frac{\varepsilon_{T}}{1-\rho}
into equation
60
above, and redefining
Z
into
Z:=\frac{1}{1-\rho}\left(\varepsilon_{\mu}\left(\frac{2}{\nu_{s}}+\frac{1}{C}\right)+\frac{\varepsilon_{abs}}{C}\right)
, we obtain that:

\mathbb{E}\min_{t\in[T]}R\left({\bm{w}}_{t}\right)\leq(1+2\rho)R(\bar{{\bm{w}}})+\varepsilon_{T}^{\prime}+Z\mu^{2}.

Further, consider an ideal case where
\bar{{\bm{w}}}
is a global minimizer of
R
over
\mathcal{B}_{0}(k):=

\left\{{\bm{w}}:\|{\bm{w}}\|_{0}\leq k\right\}
. Then
R\left({\bm{v}}_{t}\right)\geq R(\bar{{\bm{w}}})
is always true for all
t\geq 1
. It follows that the bound in equation
59
yields:

\mathbb{E}\min_{t\in[T]}\left\{(1-\rho)R\left({\bm{w}}_{t}\right)+\rho R(\bar{{\bm{w}}})\right\}\leq\mathbb{E}\min_{t\in[T]}\left\{(1-\rho)R\left({\bm{w}}_{t}\right)+\rho R\left({\bm{v}}_{t}\right)\right\}\leq R(\bar{{\bm{w}}})+\varepsilon_{T},

which implies:
\mathbb{E}\min_{t\in[T]}R\left({\bm{w}}_{t}\right)\leq R(\bar{{\bm{w}}})+\frac{\varepsilon_{T}}{1-\rho}
.
In this case, we can simply set
\rho=0.5
, and define
\varepsilon^{\prime}_{T}=\frac{\varepsilon_{T}}{1-\rho}=2\varepsilon_{T}
similarly as above. The proof is completed.

∎

F.5
Proof of Corollary
4.9

Proof of Corollary
4.9
.

Let
\varepsilon\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*}
. Let us find
T
to ensure that
\mathbb{E}\min_{t\in\{1,..,T\}}(1-\rho)R({\bm{w}}_{t})+\rho R({\bm{v}}_{t})-R(\bar{{\bm{w}}})\leq\varepsilon+Z\mu^{2}

This will be enforced if:

\displaystyle 4\alpha^{2}\frac{1}{\rho}L_{s^{\prime}}\kappa_{s}\omega^{T}\left(\|\bar{{\bm{w}}}-{\bm{w}}_{0}\|^{2}+\frac{4}{3}\frac{\eta\|\nabla R(\bar{{\bm{w}}})\|^{2}}{4\kappa_{s}L_{s^{\prime}}}\right)\leq\varepsilon

\displaystyle\iff T\log(\omega)\leq\log\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{4\alpha^{2}\frac{1}{\rho}L_{s^{\prime}}\kappa_{s}\left(\|\bar{{\bm{w}}}-{\bm{w}}_{0}\|^{2}+\frac{4}{3}\frac{\eta\|\nabla R(\bar{{\bm{w}}})\|^{2}}{4\kappa_{s}L_{s^{\prime}}}\right)}\right)

\displaystyle\iff T\geq\frac{1}{\log(\frac{1}{\omega})}\log\left(\frac{4\alpha^{2}\frac{1}{\rho}L_{s^{\prime}}\kappa_{s}\left(\|\bar{{\bm{w}}}-{\bm{w}}_{0}\|^{2}+\frac{4}{3}\frac{\eta\|\nabla R(\bar{{\bm{w}}})\|^{2}}{4\kappa_{s}L_{s^{\prime}}}\right)}{\varepsilon}\right).

Therefore, let us take:

T:=\left\lceil\frac{1}{\log(\frac{1}{\omega})}\log\left(\frac{4\alpha^{2}\frac{1}{\rho}L_{s^{\prime}}\kappa_{s}\left(\|\bar{{\bm{w}}}-{\bm{w}}_{0}\|^{2}+\frac{4}{3}\frac{\eta\|\nabla R(\bar{{\bm{w}}})\|^{2}}{4\kappa_{s}L_{s^{\prime}}}\right)}{\varepsilon}\right)\right\rceil.

(61)

We can now derive the #IZO and #HT. First, we have one hard-thresholding operation at each iteration, therefore #HT
=T
. Using the fact that
\frac{1}{\log(\frac{1}{\omega})}=\frac{1}{-\log(\omega)}=\frac{1}{-\log(1-\frac{1}{8\alpha\frac{1}{\rho}\kappa_{s}})}\leq\frac{1}{\frac{1}{8\alpha\frac{1}{\rho}\kappa_{s}}}=8\alpha\frac{1}{\rho}\kappa_{s}
(since by property of the logarithm, for all
x\in(-\infty,-1):\log(1-x)\leq-x
), and the fact that
\alpha=\frac{C}{L_{s^{\prime}}}
is independent of
\kappa_{s}
, we obtain that
\text{\#HT}=\mathcal{O}(\kappa_{s}\log\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\right))
.

We now turn to computing the #IZO. At each iteration
t
we have
q_{t}
function evaluations, therefore:

#IZO

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}q_{t}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\left(\frac{\tau}{\omega^{t}}+1\right)

\displaystyle=T+\tau\frac{\left(\frac{1}{\omega}\right)^{T}-1}{\frac{1}{\omega}-1}

\displaystyle\leq T+\frac{\tau}{{\frac{1}{\omega}-1}}\left(\frac{1}{\omega}\right)^{T}

\displaystyle=T+\frac{\tau}{{\frac{1}{\omega}-1}}\exp\left(T\log\left(\frac{1}{\omega}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\leq}1+\frac{1}{\log(\frac{1}{\omega})}\log\left(\frac{4\alpha^{2}\frac{1}{\rho}L_{s^{\prime}}\kappa_{s}\left(\|\bar{{\bm{w}}}-{\bm{w}}_{0}\|^{2}+\frac{4}{3}\frac{\eta\|\nabla R(\bar{{\bm{w}}})\|^{2}}{4\kappa_{s}L_{s^{\prime}}}\right)}{\varepsilon}\right)

\displaystyle~~~~~+\frac{\tau}{{\frac{1}{\omega}-1}}\exp\left(\log\left(\frac{1}{\omega}\right)\left[\frac{1}{\log(\frac{1}{\omega})}\log\left(\frac{4\alpha^{2}\frac{1}{\rho}L_{s^{\prime}}\kappa_{s}\left(\|\bar{{\bm{w}}}-{\bm{w}}_{0}\|^{2}+\frac{4}{3}\frac{\eta\|\nabla R(\bar{{\bm{w}}})\|^{2}}{4\kappa_{s}L_{s^{\prime}}}\right)}{\varepsilon}\right)\right.\right.

\displaystyle~~~~\left.\left.+1\right]\right)

\displaystyle=1+\frac{1}{\log(\frac{1}{\omega})}\log\left(\frac{4\alpha^{2}\frac{1}{\rho}L_{s^{\prime}}\kappa_{s}\left(\|\bar{{\bm{w}}}-{\bm{w}}_{0}\|^{2}+\frac{4}{3}\frac{\eta\|\nabla R(\bar{{\bm{w}}})\|^{2}}{4\kappa_{s}L_{s^{\prime}}}\right)}{\varepsilon}\right)

\displaystyle~~~~+\frac{\frac{\tau}{\omega}}{{\frac{1}{\omega}-1}}\frac{4\alpha^{2}\frac{1}{\rho}L_{s^{\prime}}\kappa_{s}\left(\|\bar{{\bm{w}}}-{\bm{w}}_{0}\|^{2}+\frac{4}{3}\frac{\eta\|\nabla R(\bar{{\bm{w}}})\|^{2}}{4\kappa_{s}L_{s^{\prime}}}\right)}{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=1+\frac{1}{\log(\frac{1}{\omega})}\log\left(\frac{4\alpha^{2}\frac{1}{\rho}L_{s^{\prime}}\kappa_{s}\left(\|\bar{{\bm{w}}}-{\bm{w}}_{0}\|^{2}+\frac{4}{3}\frac{\eta\|\nabla R(\bar{{\bm{w}}})\|^{2}}{4\kappa_{s}L_{s^{\prime}}}\right)}{\varepsilon}\right)

\displaystyle~~~~+\frac{\tau}{{1-\omega}}\frac{4\alpha^{2}\frac{1}{\rho}L_{s^{\prime}}\kappa_{s}\left(\|\bar{{\bm{w}}}-{\bm{w}}_{0}\|^{2}+\frac{4}{3}\frac{\eta\|\nabla R(\bar{{\bm{w}}})\|^{2}}{4\kappa_{s}L_{s^{\prime}}}\right)}{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=1+\frac{1}{\log(\frac{1}{\omega})}\log\left(\frac{4\alpha^{2}\frac{1}{\rho}L_{s^{\prime}}\kappa_{s}\left(\|\bar{{\bm{w}}}-{\bm{w}}_{0}\|^{2}+\frac{4}{3}\frac{\eta\|\nabla R(\bar{{\bm{w}}})\|^{2}}{4\kappa_{s}L_{s^{\prime}}}\right)}{\varepsilon}\right)

\displaystyle~~~~+\tau\frac{32\alpha^{3}\frac{1}{\rho^{2}}L_{s^{\prime}}\kappa_{s}^{2}\left(\|\bar{{\bm{w}}}-{\bm{w}}_{0}\|^{2}+\frac{4}{3}\frac{\eta\|\nabla R(\bar{{\bm{w}}})\|^{2}}{4\kappa_{s}L_{s^{\prime}}}\right)}{\varepsilon},

where (a) follows from equation
61
.

And we recall that
\tau=16\kappa_{s}\frac{\varepsilon_{F}}{(\alpha-1)}
, which implies that:

\tau\frac{32\alpha^{3}\frac{1}{\rho^{2}}L_{s^{\prime}}\kappa_{s}^{2}\left(\|\bar{{\bm{w}}}-{\bm{w}}_{0}\|^{2}+\frac{4}{3}\frac{\eta\|\nabla R(\bar{{\bm{w}}})\|^{2}}{2\gamma\kappa_{s}L_{s^{\prime}}}\right)}{\varepsilon}=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\varepsilon_{F}}{\varepsilon}\left(\kappa_{s}^{3}L_{s^{\prime}}+\frac{\kappa_{s}}{\nu_{s}}\right)\right).

Therefore, overall, the IZO (query complexity) is in
\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\varepsilon_{F}}{\varepsilon}\kappa_{s}^{3}L_{s^{\prime}}\right)
. The proof is completed.
∎

Appendix G
Differences Between Two-Step Projection and Euclidean Projection

In this section, we describe the differences between the two-step projection and the Euclidean projection onto the mixed constraints
\Gamma\cap\mathcal{B}_{0}(k)
. One can encounter several possible cases:

•

Case (i):
the two-step projection (2SP) and the Euclidean projection onto
\Gamma\cap\mathcal{B}_{0}(k)
are identical (see e.g. Remark
3.1
): in that case, the contribution of our paper are on the theoretical side: Theorems
3.7
,
4.3
, and
4.8
give global convergence guarantee which therefore in this case apply to the usual (non-convex) projected gradient descent algorithm with Euclidean projection.

•

Case (ii):
the 2SP and the Euclidean projection onto the mixed constraints are different: this case can be declined into several sub-cases as described below:

–

Case (a): the Euclidean projection onto the mixed constraint
\Gamma\cap\mathcal{B}_{0}(k)
is unknown: in that case, the 2SP can allow to fill such gap, since the 2SP only requires the knowledge of the projection onto
\Gamma
, which is often known and easy to do.

–

Case (b): the Euclidean projection onto the mixed constraint
\Gamma\cap\mathcal{B}_{0}(k)
is known, but computationally expensive: in that case, the 2SP can provide a simpler and faster alternative to the Euclidean projection, while still enjoying some convergence guarantees as shown in this paper.

–

Case (c): the Euclidean projection onto the mixed constraint
\Gamma\cap\mathcal{B}_{0}(k)
is known and is efficient enough (e.g. when
\Gamma
belongs to the set of positive symmetric sets such as in
(
Lu 2015
)
). In such cases, it is unclear whether the 2SP can improve upon Euclidean projection since,
at the iteration level, using the Euclidean projection is optimal (indeed, a (Euclidean) projected gradient descent step minimizes a quadratic upper bound on the objective value under constraints (derived from the smoothness of
R
)), and the 2SP is therefore suboptimal in that sense (at the iteration level).

Appendix H
Experiments

In this section, we provide some experiments to validate experimentally our theoretical results. Our experiments take 2h30 to run in total on a MacBook Pro with a 2.6GHz 6-core Intel Core i7 and 16GB of memory. Before describing our experiments, we provide a short discussion about the settings and algorithms that we will illustrate. For constraints
\Gamma
for which the Euclidean projection onto
\mathcal{B}_{0}(k)\cap\Gamma
has a closed form equal to the 2SP, our algorithm is identical to a vanilla non-convex projected gradient descent baseline (see Remark
3.1
). In such case, our contribution in this paper is on the theoretical side, by providing some global guarantees on the optimization, instead of the local guarantees from existing work (cf. Table 1).
Additionally, there are case in which there exists a closed form for projection onto
\Gamma\cap\mathcal{B}_{0}(k)
, different from the 2SP (e.g. when
\Gamma=\mathbb{R}_{+}^{d}
, cf.
(
Lu 2015
)
). Although our framework allows us to get approximate global convergence results when using the 2SP, still, at the iteration level, a gradient step followed by Euclidean projection (not 2SP) is optimal, since it minimizes a constrained quadratic upper bound on
R
. Therefore, we may not expect much improvement of the 2SP over the Euclidean projection in such case, except on the computational side. With this in mind, we provide below the outline of our experiments:

•

In Section
H.1
, we illustrate on a synthetic example the trade-off between sparsity and optimality that is introduced by the extra constraint
\Gamma
, and that is balanced by the parameter
\rho
.

•

In Section
H.2
, we consider a portfolio index tracking problem where the goal is to illustrate a real-life application of our methods.

•

In section
H.3
, we consider a multi-class logistic regression on a real life dataset, to illustrate in more details in particular the stochastic and the zeroth-order versions of our method.

For reproducibility, our code is available at
https://github.com/wdevazelhes/2SP_icml2025
.

H.1
Synthetic Experiments: Illustrating the Sparsity/Optimality Trade-Off

In the section below, we provide a synthetic experiment to illustrate our Theorem
3.7
, i.e. the trade-off between sparsity and optimality that is introduced by the extra constraint
\Gamma
, and that is balanced by
\rho\in(0,0.5]
.
We consider the synthetic linear regression example from
(
Axiotis & Sviridenko 2022
)
(Section E), with the risk below:

R({\bm{w}}):=\frac{1}{n}\left\|\bm{X}{\bm{w}}-{\bm{y}}\right\|_{2}^{2},

and where
\bm{X}
is diagonal with:

\displaystyle\bm{X}_{ii}=\begin{cases}1&\text{if $i\in I_{1}$}\\
\sqrt{\kappa}&\text{if $i\in I_{2}$}\\
1&\text{if $i\in I_{3}$}\,,\end{cases}

where
I_{1}=[s],I_{2}=[s+1,s(\kappa+1)],I_{3}=[s(\kappa+1)+1,s(\kappa^{2}+\kappa+1)]
for some
s\geq 1
and
\kappa\geq 1
(we choose
s=50
and
\kappa=2
, which results in having
d=350
),
n
denotes the number of rows of
\bm{X}
, and
{\bm{y}}
is defined as

\displaystyle y_{i}=\begin{cases}\kappa\sqrt{1-4\delta}&\text{if $i\in I_{1}$}\\
\sqrt{\kappa}\sqrt{1-2\delta}&\text{if $i\in I_{2}$}\\
1&\text{if $i\in I_{3}$}\end{cases}

for some small
\delta>0
used for tie-breaking (we set it to
1e-4
). We chose such an example as it is used by
(
Axiotis & Sviridenko 2022
)
to prove a lower bound on the fundamental trade-off between sparsity and optimality proper to IHT: they use it to show that the relaxation of the sparsity
k
, of the order
k=\Omega(\kappa^{2}\bar{k})
(see also Table
1
) is in fact unavoidable for IHT-type algorithms.

Case without Extra Constraints.

First, we illustrate our Theorem
3.4
which considers vanilla IHT, without extra constraints. In Figure
2
, on the one hand, we plot in blue, for every
k\in[d]
, the value of
R(\hat{{\bm{w}}}_{k})
where
\hat{{\bm{w}}}_{k}
is the result of running vanilla IHT with sparsity
k
up to convergence. Then, on the other hand, we go through every value of
\bar{k}\in[d]
, and for each of them, we plot a point
(K(\bar{k}),R(\bar{{\bm{w}}}_{\bar{k}}))
, where
K(\bar{k})
denotes the value of
k
required in our Theorem
3.4
, i.e.:
K(\bar{k}):=4\kappa^{2}\bar{k}
, and
\bar{{\bm{w}}}_{\bar{k}}:=\min_{{\bm{w}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:\|{\bm{w}}\|_{0}\leq\bar{k}}R({\bm{w}})
. Therefore, each of such point
R(\bar{{\bm{w}}}_{\bar{k}})
constitutes an upper bound on the value of
R(\hat{{\bm{w}}}_{K(\bar{k})})
, as we can indeed observe on Figure
2
.

Figure 2:
Illustration of Theorem
3.4
(i.e.
\Gamma=\mathbb{R}^{d}
).

Case with Extra Constraints.

We now illustrate the influence of the extra constraint
\Gamma
on the problem. We consider for
\Gamma
an
\ell_{\infty}
norm constraint of radius
\lambda>0
, that is:
\Gamma=\{{\bm{w}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:\forall i\in[d],|w_{i}|\leq\lambda\}
. In this new setting, we also go through every value of
\bar{k}\in[d]
, but this time, each of those values actually defines a curve parameterized by
\rho
, according to our Theorem
3.7
: for each
\bar{k}
we plot the parametric curve
(K(\bar{k},\rho),(1+2\rho)R(\bar{{\bm{w}}}_{\bar{k}}))
, where, similarly as above,
K(\bar{k},\rho)
denotes the required value of
k
according to Theorem
3.7
(i.e.,
K(\bar{k},\rho)=\frac{4(1-\rho)^{2}\bar{k}\kappa^{2}}{\rho^{2}}
), and
\bar{{\bm{w}}}_{\bar{k}}:=\min_{{\bm{w}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:\|{\bm{w}}\|_{0}\leq\bar{k}}R({\bm{w}})
, and where
\rho
ranges in
(0,0.5]
. We present the results for several values of
\lambda
in Figure
7
below. Note

that a priori
, the curves are allowed to cross, i.e. for a given
k
on the x-axis, one could have a point from a curve of small
\bar{k}
(i.e. lighter shade of red) which could potentially also belong to a curve of larger
\bar{k}
(let us denote it
\bar{k}^{\prime}
) (darker shade of red), which would necessarily have a larger
\rho
(let us denote it
\rho^{\prime}
), but for which the overall
(1+2\rho^{\prime})R(\bar{{\bm{w}}}_{\bar{k}^{\prime}})
could be equal to
(1+2\rho)R(\bar{{\bm{w}}}_{\bar{k}})
(since the problem will be less constrained with
\bar{k}^{\prime}
than with
k
). However, interestingly, this is not the case here due to the simplicity of the structure of the example. We can also observe that similarly as in the case where
\Gamma=\mathbb{R}^{d}
, the bound is a bit tighter in the small
k
regime (i.e. when
k\in[50,100]
).

Figure 3:
\lambda=0.1

Figure 4:
\lambda=0.5

Figure 5:
\lambda=1

Figure 6:
\lambda=2

Figure 7:
Illustration of Theorem
3.4
(with
\Gamma
an
\ell_{\infty}
ball of radius
\lambda
).

H.2
Real Data Experiment: Portfolio Index Tracking

We now consider the following index tracking problem, originally presented in
(
Takeda et al. 2013
)
, and used as well in
(
Lu 2015
;
Beck & Hallak 2016
)
. It is also similar to the portfolio optimization problem presented in
(
Kyrillidis et al. 2013
)
. We seek to reproduce the performance of an index fund (such as S
\&
P500), by investing only in a few key
k
assets, in order to limit transaction costs. The general problem can be formulated as a linear regression problem:

\min_{{\bm{w}}\in\mathcal{B}_{0}(k)\cap\Gamma}\|\bm{A}{\bm{w}}-{\bm{y}}\|^{2}

(62)

where
{\bm{w}}
represents the amount invested in each asset. For each
i\in[n]
denoting a timestep
, the
i
-th row of
\bm{A}
denotes the returns of the
d
stocks at timestep
i
, and
y_{i}
the return of the index fund.
In our scenario, we seek to limit to a value
D>0
the amount of transactions in each of
c
activity sector (group) of the portfolio (e.g. Industrials, Healthcare, etc.), denoted as
G_{i}
for
i\in[c]
. We ensure such constraint through an
\ell_{1}
norm constraint on each group:
\Gamma=\{{\bm{w}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:\forall i\in[c],\|{\bm{w}}_{G_{i}}\|_{1}\leq D\}
, where
{\bm{w}}_{G_{i}}
is the restriction of
{\bm{w}}
to group
G_{i}
(i.e. for
j\in[d]
,
{w_{G_{i}}}_{j}=w_{j}
if
j\in G_{i}
and
0
otherwise).
In our case,
{\bm{y}}
denotes the daily returns of a given portfolio index (e.g. S&P500) for a given time period (e.g. a given year), and
\bm{A}
the returns of the corresponding
d
assets (over
c
sectors) of the index during such period.

Baselines.

Up to our knowledge, there is no efficient closed form for the Euclidean projection onto
\mathcal{B}_{0}(k)\cap\Gamma
(although such projection could be done by enumerating all sparse supports sets and computing the restricted projection onto those sets, such method would have exponential complexity), but the two-step projection can easily be done by projecting onto the
\ell_{1}
ball for each sector independently. We compare our algorithm (FG-HT-2SP) to two naive baselines: (a) the first one. called "
\text{PGD}(\Gamma)+\text{final}\Pi_{\mathcal{B}_{0}}
", consists in only ensuring the constraints in
\Gamma
, followed at the end of training by a simple hard-thresholding step to keep the
k
largest components of
{\bm{w}}
in absolute value, and (b) the second one, called "
\text{PGD}(\mathcal{B}_{0})+\text{final}\Pi_{\Gamma}
", consists in running vanilla IHT, followed at the end of training by a simple projection onto
\Gamma
to keep
{\bm{w}}
in
\Gamma\cap\mathcal{B}_{0}
. We learn the weights of the portfolio on 80% of the considered period, and evaluate the out of sample (test set) performance on the remaining 20% (shaded area in the figure).

Datasets.

We compare our algorithms on three portfolio indices datasets:

•

S&P500
: We take
k=15
and
D=50
.
{\bm{y}}
denotes the daily returns from January 1, 2021, to December 31, 2022, and
\bm{A}
denotes the returns of the corresponding
d=497
assets (over
c=11
sectors). We plot our results in Figure
11
.

•

HSI
: We take
k=15
and
D=1000
.
{\bm{y}}
denotes the daily returns from January 1, 2021, to December 31, 2022, and
\bm{A}
denotes the returns of the corresponding
d=72
assets (over
c=4
sectors). We plot our results in Figure
11
.

•

CSI300
: We take
k=15
and
D=100
.
{\bm{y}}
denotes the daily returns from March 1, 2021 (due to missing values in early 2021), to December 31, 2022, and
\bm{A}
denotes the returns of the corresponding
d=291
assets (over
c=10
sectors). We plot our results in Figure
11
.

The data for those three indices is scrapped from the web using the
beautifulsoup
1
1

1

https://pypi.org/project/beautifulsoup4/
library to gather information about the index, and the
yfinance
2
2

2

https://github.com/ranaroussi/yfinance
library to scrap the returns of such stocks during the considered time period. We provide in Table
2
below the respective dimensions of the train-sets used for the experiments (which constitutes, as we recall, 80% of the total dataset).

INDEX

{\bm{n}}

{\bm{d}}

S&P500

402

497

CSI300

353

291

HSI

394

72

Table 2:
Number of samples (
n
) and dimension (
d
) of the training sets for the index tracking experiment

Results.

As we can observe on Figure
11
, overall, the true index (blue curve) is more successfully tracked by our method (FG-HT-2SP, green curve), on the train-set of S&P500 and CSI300 and on the test-set of HSI and CSI300.
Additionally, we have observed that for S&P500, our algorithm solution non-zero weights spans
9
of the
11
sectors for the S&P500 index,
7
sectors out of
10
for the CSI300 index, and
3
of the
4
sectors the one for the HSI index. Therefore, such portfolios are well diversified, as successfully enforced by our constraint.

Index tracking with sector constraints for various indices

On the Verification of Assumptions
2.1
to
2.3
:

Note that such index tracking experiments verify Assumptions
2.1
,
2.2
and
2.3
:

•

Assumption
2.1
is verified since the cost function is quadratic, with a design matrix of size
n>d
(except in the case of S&P500). As can be expected with such matrices in general, the Hessian
\bm{H}=2\bm{A}^{\top}\bm{A}
is positive-definite (we have indeed verified in our code that it is).
Therefore the RSC constant is bounded below by
\lambda_{\min}
where
\lambda_{\min}
is the smallest eigenvalue of
2\bm{A}^{\top}\bm{A}
. Note that for S&P500, strong convexity is not verified since
d>n
: however, since we take
k=15
, with high probability (i.e. unless we can find
s=2k=30
columns of
\bm{A}
that are exactly linearly dependent), RSC should be verified.

•

Assumption
2.2
and Assumption
4.5
are both verified since the cost function is quadratic, therefore the (strong) RSS constant is bounded above by
2\|\bm{A}\|_{s}^{2}
, where
\|\cdot\|_{s}
denotes the spectral norm.

•

Definition
2.3
is verified since projection onto
\Gamma
can be done group-wise, and for each group the projection is onto an
\ell_{1}
ball, which is a convex symmetric set (which is support-preserving from Remark
2.4
), therefore, overall,
\Gamma
is support-preserving).

H.3
Real Data Experiment: Multiclass Logistic Regression

We now consider the multiclass logistic regression problem with class group-wise
\ell_{2}
norm constraint as follows. We have
R_{i}({\bm{w}})=\sum_{j=1}^{c}\left[\frac{\lambda}{c}\left\|{\bm{w}}_{j}\right\|_{2}^{2}-\mathbf{1}\left\{y_{i}=j\right\}\log\frac{\exp\left({\bm{x}}_{i}^{\top}{\bm{w}}_{j}\right)}{\sum_{l=1}^{c}\exp\left({\bm{x}}_{i}^{\top}{\bm{w}}_{l}\right)}\right]
, where
y_{i}
is the target output of
{\bm{x}}_{i}
,
c
is the number of classes, and
{\bm{w}}_{j}
is the weight vector specific to class
j
. In addition to the sparsity constraint
\mathcal{B}_{0}(k)
, we enforce the following additional constraint
\Gamma=\{{\bm{w}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:\forall j\in[c]:\|{\bm{w}}_{j}\|_{2}\leq D\}
, for some constant
D\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
, where
d=p\times c
, with
p
the number of features of the samples
{\bm{x}}_{i}
. More precisely, in such multiclass logistic regression, we seek to ensure an extra regularization not only on the whole global weight vector
{\bm{w}}
(with the used squared
\ell_{2}
penalty), but also on each weight vector related to each class (through
\Gamma
), in order to prevent a potential class-wise overfitting.

Up to our knowledge, there is no known closed form for the Euclidean projection onto such
\Gamma\cap\mathcal{B}_{0}(k)
. However, the two-step projection (2SP) can be done easily: once the first projection is done (projection onto
\mathcal{B}_{0}(k)
, i.e. hard-thresholding) and the sparse support
S
is identified as per Section
3.1
, the projection onto
\Gamma
restricted to
S
can be easily done since
\Gamma
is class-wise decomposable, and therefore it suffices to project, for each
j\in[c]
, each
{\bm{w}}_{j}
onto the
\ell_{2}
ball of radius
D
.

We have the smoothness constant
L
as below (see
(
Böhning 1992
)
for a derivation):

L=\sigma_{\max}\left(\frac{1}{2n}\left(\bm{I}_{c\times c}-\frac{1}{c}\bm{1}_{c}\bm{1}_{c}^{\top}\right)\otimes\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+2\lambda\bm{I}_{d\times d}\right)

(63)

Where
\otimes
denotes the Kronecker product,
\sigma_{\max}
the largest singular value of a matrix,
\bm{I}_{m\times m}
the identity matrix of size
m\times m
for some
m
, and
\bm{1}_{c}
the vector
[1,1,..,1]^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{c}
.

We consider the
dna
dataset from the LibSVM dataset repository
(
Chang & Lin 2011
)
, and we choose
D=0.5
,
\lambda=10
. For the stochastic case we take
B=1e^{5}
, and for the stochastic and ZO case we take
\alpha=2
. Note that in the stochastic case, if the growing batch-size required by Theorem
4.3
becomes larger than
n
, we keep it fixed to
n
(i.e. in such case we take the whole dataset at each step). In the zeroth-order case, we take
\mu=1e-6
. We set set all other hyperparameters as per Theorems
3.7
,
4.3
and
4.8
.
In Figures
15
,
19
,
23
and
27
, we plot the number of calls to a gradient
\nabla R_{i}
(IFO: iterative first order oracle), and number of hard-thresholding operations (NHT), for various values of
k
and
D
(for the zeroth-order case, we plot the IZO (number of calls to the function
R
) instead of the IFO).
We can observe that HSG-HT-2SP allows a smaller IFO than FG-HT-2SP in early iterations, since it does not need to compute a full gradient at each iteration.

In addition, to illustrate the theoretical improvement of our results on zeroth-order, even in the case where there is no additional constraint, we compare in Figures
30
,
33
and
36
our algorithm HZO-HT with ZOHT
(
de Vazelhes et al. 2022
)
, choosing for both algorithm an initial number of random direction as prescribed by our Theorem
4.8
, and choosing, for the learning rate, in our case the one prescribed by Theorem
4.8
, and for ZOHT, the one prescribed by Theorem 1 from
(
de Vazelhes et al. 2022
)
(and in both cases we fix
s=3k
as per Theorem
4.8
): we can see that, in addition to being able to obtain a convergence in risk without system error, contrary to ZOHT (cf. Table
1
), our Theorem
4.8
also prescribes a better (larger) learning rate (i.e. less conservative), leading to faster convergence.

On the Verification of Assumptions
2.1
to
2.3
:

Note that such logistic regression experiments verify Assumptions
2.1
,
2.2
,
4.5
and
2.3
:

•

Assumption
2.1
is verified thanks to the added squared
\ell_{2}
regularization, which makes the problem strongly convex and hence also restricted strongly convex.

•

Assumption
2.2
and Assumption
4.5
are both verified since the problem is smooth with a constant
L
as described above in equation
63
, and therefore such constant is also a valid (strong) restricted-smoothness constant.

•

Definition
2.3
is verified since, since, similarly as in the index tracking experiments from Section
H.2
, projection onto
\Gamma
can be done group-wise, and for each group the projection is onto an
\ell_{1}
ball, which is a convex sign-free set (which is support-preserving from Remark
2.4
), therefore, overall,
\Gamma
is support-preserving.
